Net: any save on that dialog — even a rename — wrote back the flag as it was when the page loaded. A lost update on a gate that fails **open**.
- **Fix = the `send_paused` pattern.** `supports_api_send` is stripped from `providerCreateSchema`/`providerUpdateSchema` (the bulk PATCH cannot carry it even if a client sends it) and moved to `POST /api/providers/[providerId]/api-send` `{enabled, reason?}`, which writes an audit row naming the actor. New providers are created with the gate **off**. `ProviderForm` still *reads* the value via a new display-only `supportsApiSend` prop (it gates the sending-hours block) but can no longer write it. UI control sits on the provider detail page: **enabling confirms, disabling applies directly** (fails safe).
- **Migration 0131** widens `send_circuit_events_event_check` to add `api_send_enabled` / `api_send_disabled` — reusing the existing per-provider send-posture audit rather than adding a table. Additive, writes no rows, inert until the endpoint ships; mirrors 0049's CHECK widening. **The gate previously had NO audit trail, and `sms_providers` has no `updated_at`** — which is exactly why the reversion couldn't be attributed to an actor or a time.
- **⚠️ Second defect found and fixed: `.partial()` does NOT strip an inner `.default()`.** Verified against this repo's Zod, not assumed: parsing `{name:"x"}` against `providerCreateSchema.partial()` yielded `short_link_supported: false` — a real value, so the route's `if (v === undefined) continue` guard didn't skip it and it was **written**. Any partial PATCH silently cleared that flag. Re-declared on the update schema without the default, so "omitted" means "leave unchanged" while an **explicit** `false` is still written. [scripts/test-provider-update-schema.ts](../scripts/test-provider-update-schema.ts) (16 assertions) pins both defects and asserts on what the route would actually SET, not just what Zod returns.

## 2026-08-13 — Tells.co Phase 2: send path (`tls`) — docs: 04-features/sms-send-pipeline, 06-integrations, spec §7/§9, CHANGELOG
- **`tellsAdapter.send()` implemented** per the verified §5.1 contract: `POST` only, form-encoded `key/from/to/message/metadata`, bare 11-digit numbers, 15s timeout. Still inert — `tls.supports_api_send` stays `false` until Phase 5.
- **⚠️ Classification reads the BODY, not the HTTP status.** Every Tells send error — including a totally invalid API key — returns **HTTP 200** with `{"status":"error"}`. A status-only classifier reads total auth failure as success. Success is `status==="queued"` **and** a usable `id`. Split into a pure `classifyTellsSend(httpStatus, rawBody)` so the matrix is unit-testable without a network — [scripts/test-tells-send.ts](../scripts/test-tells-send.ts), **41 assertions** over verbatim probe fixtures.
- **`ok: true` is never returned without a `messageId`** — the drain keys its `sent` bucket off `res.ok` alone, so a `queued`-with-no-id response must normalize to a failure or a row gets marked sent with a null message id.
- **`metadata` threaded end-to-end.** New additive optional `NormalizedSendParams.metadata` / `Sender.metadata`; the drain populates `{ stage_send_id }` for every send and mirrors it into the redacted `send_attempts` audit string. It is the **only** correlation handle a Tells DLR carries (no callback URL, no `?ss=`), and it comes back **lowercase** and always as a **string**.
- **`sms_count` → `segmentsCount`**; multi-segment does not fragment the DLR (one `id`), unlike Ahoi. `id` is coerced to string — it is a string on the send response but a **number** on the webhook.
- **Q5 CLOSED** (Dmytro, 2026-08-13) — not by probing Tells but by ruling it out: CamMan excludes a contact from a stage it has already been sent, so the 429 duplicate refusal is unreachable in normal operation. It therefore stays non-retryable with a distinct `providerStatus="duplicate"` marker **and logs at `console.error`** — in production it would indicate a **CamMan-side dedup bug**, not provider behaviour. Deliberately NOT a fifth `AttemptClassification` value: that column is CHECK-constrained (0064) and feeds the reports enum.
- No retry on timeout (may have landed ⇒ `indeterminate`). New optional env var `TELLS_API_BASE_URL`.

## 2026-08-13 — Tells.co Phase 1: `tells_webhook_events` (migration 0130, FILED NOT APPLIED) + `tls` adapter skeleton — docs: 03-data-model + ERD, CHANGELOG
- **Migration 0130 creates `tells_webhook_events`** — one append-only table with a `kind` (`dlr`\|`inbound`) discriminator, deliberately unlike the two-table `ahoi_*`/`textrequest_*` pattern: Tells has no replay and no reconciliation API, so this table is the only copy of an event AND the only retry surface. Partial UNIQUE(`provider_id`,`dedup_key`) WHERE NOT NULL + `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` bumping `duplicate_count` — a redelivery is a counter bump, never an error, because an INSERT that can fail is an event that can be lost. Design + verified contract: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design.md` §4/§5.1. (ClickUp 869egfjx2.)
- **⚠️ Renumbered from the spec's "0129" to 0130** — 0129 was taken by `segment_rules_sent_from_phone` while Tells was parked. The spec's number was stale; verified against `origin/main` before writing rather than trusted.
- **`tls` adapter registered** (`lib/sends/providers/tells.ts`) — Phase 1 skeleton: `send()` returns a clean not-implemented failure, `parseDlr`/`parseInbound` return `null`. `toTellsRecipient` (bare 11-digit, per probe A1) and `tellsPhoneToE164` are real, since Phase 3's opt-out path needs the inverse. The eight probe-derived traps are documented at the top of the file so Phase 2/3 can't miss them.
- **This fixes a check that is currently RED on `main`.** `tls.supports_api_send` is `true` in production while no `tls` adapter exists, so `scripts/test-provider-registry-db-keys.ts` fails — reproduced by reverting registry.ts to main's version. Consequence on main today: a stage assigned to Tells is accepted, activated (audience snapshotted) and scheduled, then refused at drain with `unknown_provider`. With the adapter registered it refuses legibly instead — but per the spec that flag should still be `false` until Phase 5.
- Schema mirrored into `db/schema.ts` (`tells_webhook_events` + `TellsWebhookEvent`/`NewTellsWebhookEvent`). Journal entry 130 + snapshot cloned forward (id/prevId only), per the hand-authored-migration convention.

## 2026-08-13 — Segment rule rows stay on one line: `MultiSelectPicker` gains an inline chip layout — docs: 04-features/ui-system, CHANGELOG
- Picking a phone number on a `sent_from_provider_phone` rule put its chip on a second row beneath the trigger, which made that control taller than its siblings — and because the rule row centres its items, every other dropdown shifted off the shared baseline. `components/multi-select-picker.tsx` gains `layout="inline"` (chips beside the trigger, `flex-nowrap`) plus `triggerClassName`; the segment Rules row opts in with `maxChipsShown={3}`. Default `"stacked"` is unchanged, so every existing full-width form caller is untouched.
- Measured after: adding chips leaves the row at 66px and the other controls' positions **identical** — the block widens instead (chips box 258px at 3 chips).

## 2026-08-12 — Segment rule: "Sent from phone number" (0129) — docs: 03-data-model, 04-features/audience-segments, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- New rule type `sent_from_provider_phone`, value `{provider_id, phone_ids[]}`, operators is/is_not. Counts only `stage_sends.status='sent'`, matching the reports. Eval is a single index-only scan of `stage_sends_org_provider_phone_sent_idx` (0129).
- Backfilled 1,008,689 pre-0112 `stage_sends.provider_phone_id` rows from their stage (100% resolvable, 3 numbers) so the rule sees the full send history rather than only post-2026-07-20 sends.
- Fixed a latent bug from 0098: set-shaped values (`phone_type`, `carrier`) were rejected by `verifyValueOwnership` and dropped by `isRuleComplete`. Both rule types were uncreatable; prod had zero.

## 2026-08-12 — Tells.co **Phase 0 CLOSED**: verified payload contract (§5.1), STOP self-healing closed as won't-build — docs: superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design (§2/§4.5/§4.6/§5/§5.1/§7/§8/§9/§10), CHANGELOG
- **§5.1 is the new contract and supersedes §2**, which was the pre-probe claim and was **wrong in eight places**. Ran ~11 billable messages (~$0.14) against provider 855 / credential 712 / TFN +18445694179. (ClickUp 869egfjx2.)
- **Corrections that would each have caused a silent bug:** `metadata` is **lowercase** on an otherwise PascalCase DLR (reading `Metadata` returns undefined on every callback — the field Phase 2's whole correlation depends on); it is always present as `null`, never omitted; it always returns as a **string** even when a JSON object was sent; the status webhook carries **no `Key` field at all**; `Id`/`To`/`From` are **numbers on webhooks but strings on the send response**; two date formats (`Z` on DLRs, `+00:00` elsewhere).
- **"No webhook retries" is false.** Tells retries a non-2xx **4 times at 60s intervals (~3 min), then abandons the message's remaining statuses** — a sustained failure costs the terminal `delivered`, not just the event you rejected. **`Date` is the delivery-ATTEMPT timestamp** and advances on every retry, so it must never enter a dedup key; `(Id, Status)` verified stable across 4 redeliveries.
- **"No idempotency key on send" is false** — byte-identical repeats get HTTP 429 duplicate detection (new Q5: unknown window could block two stages sending one creative to the same contact).
- **A bad API key returns HTTP 200** with the failure only in the body ⇒ Tells is Ahoi-shaped; classify off the body. Multi-segment sends produce **one** id (no Ahoi-style DLR fragmentation). `Timezone: "UTC"` verified truthful.
- **STOP-undelivered self-healing CLOSED as won't-build (§8).** Probe C3 returned `ErrorMessage: "Network Error"` — indistinguishable from a transient carrier failure. Auto-suppressing on it would silently and permanently remove reachable, consenting contacts on every delivery hiccup. Not blocked on a threshold or approval: **the signal does not exist at this provider.**
- **Consequence (§4.5):** the inbound webhook is now the only automated STOP path, so the silence monitors are the **sole detection layer** for broken STOP intake — priority raised, Phase 4 doesn't ship without them. The DLR-coverage monitor must count **2 events per success (`sent`+`delivered`) and 1 per failure (`undelivered` alone)** or it fires on every genuine failure and gets muted.
- **New hard Phase 3 requirement (§4.6):** the inbound body carries the **full live API key** in `Key`, so `raw_body` capture must redact that one field before persisting. Verbatim everywhere else. Independent of the rotation decision — a credential in a DB backup is a different exposure class from one in a log.
- **API-key rotation: considered and DECLINED** (recorded with reasoning in §5). Capture-route deletion **moved from the Phase 0 exit to the Phase 3 exit** — it stays live for the build.
- `scripts/probe-tells-api.ts`: classification verdict now derives from **what A3 actually did**, not from the spread of status codes. The old heuristic drew the exactly-wrong conclusion on the first live run (saw A8's 429 among the 200s, reported "Text Request-shaped" when A3's 200-with-body-error proved the opposite). Transport failures (`HTTP 0`) are now reported as our side failing, not as provider behaviour.

## 2026-08-12 — Provider credentials: API key is the baseline for ALL provider types; STOP-callback button gated; Tells probe reads key+number from the DB — docs: 07-conventions, superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design §5, CHANGELOG
- **Decision (ClickUp 869egmakh open decision #2):** an API Key field is the **baseline for every provider type**; email/password and other login shapes are **per-provider extras**, never the baseline. The full dynamic form renderer stays in 869egmakh's scope.
- **Recon correction:** the universal API-key field already existed and was already provider-agnostic, write-only and on the encrypted path — there is **no email+password provider credential anywhere in the app** (the only other `type="password"` inputs are CamMan's own Supabase auth pages). What read as single-provider was the **copy**: the Add/Rotate placeholders hardcoded `Paste the TextHub api_key` on every provider's page. They now interpolate the provider row's `name`. **No migration** — `provider_credentials` has carried `api_key_encrypted` / `api_key_last4` / `label` since 0110.
- **Bug fixed: the "STOP callback" button rendered for every provider** while its route imports `registerOptOutCallback` from `lib/sends/texthub-optout` and registers a `/api/webhooks/texthub/opt-out/<token>` URL. From an Ahoi / Text Request / Tells account it offered to register a **TextHub** callback using that account's key. Now gated to `txh`/`txh2`, matching how Send test and Check connection were already gated. Text Request's own `register-textrequest-hooks` still has no button — tracked on 869egmakh P2.
- **`scripts/probe-tells-api.ts` now resolves the key and sending number from the DATABASE** instead of `TELLS_API_KEY`/`TELLS_FROM` env vars: the key is pasted through the Accounts UI (encrypted), the TFN is a `provider_phones` row, and the probe decrypts via the same dual-read helper the pollers use. The key is never printed — the banner shows the account label and last-4 only. Refuses to guess when a provider has multiple accounts (`TELLS_CREDENTIAL_ID`). `TELLS_TO` stays env-only: the test handset is the one value not in the DB.
- The probe banner now **warns when `supports_api_send` is true while no matching adapter is registered** — the Tells row is currently in that state, so a stage assigned to it would be accepted, activated and scheduled, then refuse at drain time with `unknown_provider`.

## 2026-08-12 — Tells.co Phase 0: A-series send-probe script (`scripts/probe-tells-api.ts`) — docs: superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design §5, CHANGELOG
- Drives probes **A1–A8** against `app.tells.co/api/sms.php`. Every call prints its **HTTP status**, response headers, elapsed ms and the raw body verbatim — A3 (bad API key) is the decisive one, since it settles whether Tells is Ahoi-shaped (always 200, classify off the body) or Text Request-shaped (real status codes), and the script says which in its closing summary. (ClickUp 869egfjx2.)
- ⚠️ **Sends real SMS.** `--dry-run` prints every request and sends nothing; the live banner names the target number and the billable count. Config via `TELLS_API_KEY` / `TELLS_FROM` / `TELLS_TO` / optional `TELLS_TEST_LINK` / `TELLS_API_URL` — all required vars fail fast with exit 1, and none have defaults, so a misconfigured run can't quietly target the wrong number.
- A2 is derived from A1a's response rather than costing a send: it prints the JS type of every success-body field, which is what actually answers "is `id` `\"3\"` or `3`?". A7b sends the real Phase 2 metadata shape (`{stage_send_id, probe}`), so its DLRs also feed probe B8.
- The API key is redacted from the printed request line only — it isn't part of the payload contract and the output gets pasted around. Response bodies print entirely unredacted, which is the point.

## 2026-08-12 — Standing rule: multi-step work happens in a throwaway git worktree, not the shared `C:/AFF/camman` checkout — docs: 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- `C:/AFF/camman` is worked in by more than one agent session at a time, and any of them can move its `HEAD` between two of your commands. Git gives no warning and no lock. A session hit this on 2026-08-12: `git branch -m <newname>` renamed **another session's branch**, because `HEAD` had been switched underneath it between the commit and the rename. Nothing was lost (a rename touches no commits and keeps upstream config), but the failure is silent and lands on someone else's work.
- New [07-conventions.md](07-conventions.md) section: branch from `origin/main` into `.claude/worktrees/<name>`, junction the shared `node_modules` rather than reinstalling, **unlink that junction with `rmdir` and never `rm -rf`** (it follows the link and would delete the main checkout's dependencies), clean up the worktree on merge, and prefer explicit two-argument git forms over "operate on current branch" ones.

## 2026-08-12 — Tells.co Phase 0: probe route gains `?status=` (probe B7) + API-key rotation scheduled into the Phase 0 exit — docs: superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design §5.0/§5/§7, CHANGELOG
- `?status=<code>` forces a non-2xx ack so probe **B7** (does Tells retry a rejected webhook?) is runnable — it previously was not, because the route always `200`d. Exact counterpart to `?delay=` and composable with it: both apply only *after* the payload is logged, so neither can cost us an event. Out-of-range values are ignored and fall back to `200`, so a typo can't turn a real capture into a rejected one. The `GET` reachability handler ignores both. (ClickUp 869egfjx2.)
- Phase 0 exit is now **three** criteria, not one: the payload contract, **rotating the Tells API key**, and deleting the capture route. The rotation moved from "if we want to be strict" to a scheduled step because it is free only until Phase 3 ships the inbound `Key` check — after that it needs coordinating with Tells's own cutover.
- Recorded the distinction the runbook depends on: rotating **our path token** means updating Tells's webhook URL *first*; rotating the **Tells API key** leaves the URL alone and instead needs the credential swapped at the moment Tells starts sending the new key. Conflating the two produces a wrong runbook step.

## 2026-08-12 — Tells.co Phase 0: TEMPORARY webhook capture route (`/api/webhooks/tells/probe-<token>`) — docs: superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design §5.0, CHANGELOG
- **Temporary, delete-on-Phase-3.** New public route logs method, headers, full URL and raw body verbatim to the console with a `[tells-probe]` prefix, returns `200` JSON, and does nothing else — no DB, no parsing, no processing. Payloads are read from Vercel runtime logs. (ClickUp 869egfjx2.)
- Replaces the originally-planned webhook.site bin: the Tells **inbound payload carries our Tells API key in its `Key` field**, so captured payloads must stay on our own infrastructure.
- Auth is the unguessable path segment only. `/api/*` is excluded from the `proxy.ts` middleware matcher, so no gating change was needed. `?delay=<ms>` (capped 55s, `maxDuration = 60`) exists solely for probe B9's slow-ack test and logs before delaying.
- **Deliberately unredacted logging** — verbatim capture is the point of Phase 0 — which means runtime logs will contain the Tells API key. Rotate after Phase 0 if we want to be strict; the spec §5.0 records why key rotation has a *different* ordering constraint from the path-token rotation in §4.3.
- Not added to `05-flows.md` on purpose: the route is ephemeral, and diagramming it would imply a permanent flow that gets deleted in Phase 3. §5.0 of the spec is its documentation surface.

## 2026-08-12 — Tells.co SMS provider (`tls`): approved design spec, build gated — docs: superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design, CHANGELOG
- Design only — **no code, no migration, no provider row**. Build is gated on txr S1 passing and ClickUp card `869e97atu` closing (still `to do` at time of writing).
- Spec: [superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design.md](superpowers/specs/2026-08-12-tells-provider-design.md). Covers the (undocumented, support-supplied) Tells API contract, a 25-item Phase 0 live-probe checklist, the `tells_webhook_events` schema (provisionally migration 0129), and the Phase 1–5 plan with approval gates.
- Core design divergence from the `ahoi_*`/`textrequest_*` two-table pattern: **one** append-only `tells_webhook_events` table with a `kind` discriminator, captured persist-first (single committed INSERT, then best-effort inline processing, then ack) and drained by a cron sweeper. Justification: Tells has no webhook retry, no replay, and **no reconciliation API of any kind**, so the table is the only copy and the only retry surface — unlike Ahoi/TR, which are both backstopped by a poll.
- Recorded gotchas: the status webhook carries **no `Key` field** (so the path token is the only viable auth gate there); `Date`/`Timezone` are captured as verbatim TEXT, never cast at capture, because TextHub claimed UTC while sending Mountain and Text Request repeated the bug.
- STOP-undelivered auto-suppression is explicitly **out of scope** pending the exact provider error text from Phase 0 and separate compliance approval.

## 2026-08-12 — Segment rules: rule-type + FK value dropdowns are now type-to-search (`<SearchableSelect>`) — docs: 04-features/ui-system, 04-features/audience-segments, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- New shared `components/searchable-select.tsx`: the single-select sibling of `MultiSelectPicker` (Radix Popover + filter input + ↑/↓/Enter nav, commits one value and closes on pick). Its trigger mirrors `<SelectTrigger>` styling so it drops into an existing row without shifting layout.
- Applied in `components/segments/rules-panel.tsx` to the **rule type** (22 options) and the **brand/offer/segment/contact-group value** pickers (up to 500 rows each), which had no search at all. Operator, AND/OR combinator, and lookback period stay plain `<Select>`s (2–8 options) — the ≤10-option threshold is now written down in 07-conventions.
- Behavior preserved: auto-save on pick (PATCH), the hydrated `ref` fallback label for values whose options haven't loaded or that are archived, and the incomplete-rule amber border. No API, schema, or eval change — UI only.
- Convention note: `SearchableSelect` is the generic single-select; `OfferPicker`/`SegmentPicker` remain the specialized pin+recent variants. The overlap is deliberate and documented in ui-system §6 — extend one of the three rather than adding a fourth.

## 2026-08-12 — txr footer wording is NOT provider-specific: inherits the system-wide `stop_text` default (`Stop to END`); only the non-empty-footer guard stays txr-specific — docs: 06-integrations, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- Removed the `TXR_OPT_OUT_FOOTER` constant. txr carries opt-out language via the same system-wide `stop_text` default as every other provider; the guard (`hasOptOutLanguage` → `missing_opt_out_language`, enforced for txr) is the only txr-specific piece. Code: `lib/sends/segments.ts`, `scripts/test-textrequest-send.ts`.

## 2026-08-12 — Opt-out footer ownership: CamMan owns the footer, TR does NOT auto-append (belief disproven at go-live) — docs: 06-integrations, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- The txr go-live's first send arrived with **no opt-out language** because the code relied on Text Request auto-appending `Text STOP to opt out`. Pulling TR's own message record proved TR did **not** append it on API `/messages` sends — the "auto-appends server-side (Phase 0)" belief was a misattribution of the TR *portal* UI's behavior.
- Policy: **CamMan owns the footer**, rendered into the body via the stage's `stop_text` (wording `Text STOP to opt out`). Removed `withProviderFooter`/`TXR_APPENDED_FOOTER`; added `hasOptOutLanguage()` + a kickoff refusal `missing_opt_out_language` — **enforced for txr**, **dry-run** (log + Telegram, no refusal) for other API providers through a 30-day window before widening. Segment counting now uses the actual rendered body (no phantom footer).
- Code: `lib/sends/segments.ts`, `lib/sends/kickoff.ts`, `lib/sends/kickoff-refusals.ts`, `lib/sends/scheduled.ts`; tests `scripts/test-textrequest-send.ts`.

## 2026-08-12 — offer report: dedup at every display grain (0128) — docs: 04-features/epc-denominator, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- The last surface still assembling a clicker count by **adding up its parts**. 0126 fixed *what* it counted; this fixes *how* it aggregates. Three summing sites removed: per-stage → campaign, campaign → (offer, group) cell, and cells → org benchmark.
- Measured before: cells **189,974 summed vs 151,386 distinct (+25.5%)**, 60 of 72 inflated; org benchmark **77,811 vs 52,142 (+49.2%)**.
- ⚠️ **The benchmark was the important half.** Inflating a denominator depresses EPC, so the benchmark's EPC was depressed harder than the rows' — **every group read ~19% better than the org average by construction**, on the screen whose only purpose is that comparison. Fixing the cells alone would have inverted the bias, not removed it.
- Structural, not incidental: 65 of 83 cells aggregate more than one campaign (largest 47), `unnest(group_ids)` replicates a campaign into every group it targeted (57 of 225 target >1), and 132,952 contacts belong to multiple groups.
- **A third summing site was found in the app layer**: the table footer summed the group rows. It now reads an offer-grain distinct count carried on the matview (`offer_clicks`), so **the Clicks column deliberately no longer foots** — a contact in two of an offer's groups is one offer clicker and two group clickers. The UI states this.
- One manual-fallback rule everywhere: `DISTINCT(tracked) + SUM(manual visits)`, with rows containing manual stages labelled **`+manual`** (33 of 80 cells). Fully-manual campaigns are 1.03% of revenue, mixed 4.45% — not worth a second rule.
- Cost measured before building: the dedup query is **578ms** against an existing **35.6s** refresh (+1.6%), and needs **no new index** — the tracked side is an Index Only Scan on `counted_clickers_campaign_idx`.
- `scripts/verify-epc-surface-grains.ts` **flipped from pinning non-compliance to asserting compliance** at all three grains, plus an assertion that the footer is *not* the sum of the cells.
- Docs now record the **time-basis difference as a design decision** rather than an open defect, with the measured reason the creative picker keeps its 30-day sort.

## 2026-08-12 — offer-report refresh: dead-man heartbeat + staleness on screen — docs: 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- The refresh cron's **failure** path was already sound (try/catch, Tier-1 Telegram alert, HTTP 500 so the scheduler flags red, per-view duration logging, `report_refresh_log` stamped only after BOTH refreshes succeed). An earlier card claimed it had no error handling; that note was stale — see the new conventions entry.
- **The gap was silence, not failure.** The catch block only runs if the route runs. A scheduler no-show left the previous numbers on screen, internally consistent and arbitrarily old, with nothing raised.
- Registered `offer-group-report-refresh` in the heartbeat registry (tolerance **26h**; twice-daily at 05:00/20:00 UTC means the worst normal age is 15h). Stamped on success only, after both refreshes.
- **Watched by the daily** `rebuild-counted-clickers` job, never by itself — a dead job cannot report itself dead. A daily watcher catches a 26h breach promptly; the weekly monitors would take up to a week.
- `/offers/[id]/report` now flags its own staleness: **>16h warn** (one refresh missed), **>26h alert** (two). A bare timestamp does not carry this — 3 days ago and 6 hours ago render identically.
- Deploy ordering checked: next refresh 20:00 UTC stamps before the watcher's 07:20 UTC check, so no false "never recorded a run" alert on the first cycle.

## 2026-08-12 — By-X reports dedup at dimension grain (was summing per-stage counts) — docs: 04-features/epc-denominator, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- **A cross-surface check found lifetime EPC disagreeing for the same campaign** by a median of 26.7% (max 47.7%). Cause: two surfaces followed the documented "dedup at the grain of the row displayed" rule and two summed per-stage counts instead. Summing double-counts anyone who clicked more than one stage, and most campaigns have five.
- **Fixed for By Number / By Offer / By Sequence**: the dimension row's denominator is now a `COUNT(DISTINCT contact_id)` at the dimension's own grain (`getCountedClickersByDimension`), not an addition of per-stage counts. Measured overcount removed: **By Number −38.6%, By Offer −27.2%, By Sequence −7.4%**.
- Cost measured before adopting: ~85ms per dimension vs ~18ms for the shared per-stage aggregate; a request renders one dimension, so **+66ms** on a route already issuing several queries.
- **Manual-mode fallback preserved**: manual stages mint no links, so the aggregate is `DISTINCT(tracked) + SUM(manual visits)` — the aggregate form of the per-stage `denominatorFor()` rule. Verified: the gap between rendered and pure-distinct is exactly the manual visit total on all three dimensions.
- ⚠️ **By Group is exempt by construction** — fractional shares split across a contact's groups have no set to dedup. Now **labelled in the UI** as not comparable with the other tabs, rather than silently different.
- **`/offers/[id]/report` remains non-compliant and is carded, not fixed here.** Its view sums per-stage counts, 23% of its campaigns use a manual fallback that cannot be deduped, and its displayed grain is (offer, group) — a bigger change than it first appeared, needing another migration on two matviews whose refresh cron still has no error handling.
- **New guard: `scripts/verify-epc-surface-grains.ts`** asserts every surface dedups at its own grain, asserts the shipped report path decomposes as `DISTINCT(tracked) + SUM(manual)`, pins the offer report's known non-compliance so a silent change fails the check, prints its input scope, and **refuses to compare across grains**. Its predecessor produced a fabricated per-campaign Creatives column by summing — the exact error it failed to catch.

## 2026-08-11 — clickers backfill: 3,022 stranded combos repaired + watermark-independent rebuild + reconciliation probe — docs: 04-features/tracking-attribution, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- **The last live defect from the Private Relay damage.** The rescore backfill corrected click classifications without touching `scored_at`, so 4,312 corrected rows fell behind the propagate watermark and 3,022 (contact, brand, offer) combos were stranded — invisible to segment clicker rules and the audience-snapshot `cl_set`. TARGETING data, not reporting.
- **Backfill applied (production write, gated):** 3,022 rows inserted, `clickers` 56,056 → 59,078, reconciliation probe 3,022 → **0**. Delta matched the dry-run prediction exactly. Second run inserts 0 (idempotent). Pre-write snapshot of 56,038 primary keys in `docs/snapshots/` (checksum `71b5986113daf254822f966bcaba3bf9`).
- **Activation gate, checked immediately before the write** — `was_clicker_at_snapshot` freezes at activation and audience immutability means a pool captured mid-backfill would bake in a partial clicker set permanently. The gate's first form measured HISTORY not in-flight work (58 "mid-materialization" stages whose newest was a month old; 53 "imminent" stages all overdue by >1h). Narrowed on evidence, not for convenience — a gate that can never read clear gets force-bypassed, which is worse than no gate.
- **`mode: 'rebuild'`** ignores the watermark and rescans all history; idempotent via the existing `NOT EXISTS` guard, and **deliberately does not advance the cursor** so the two passes stay independent. Verified in production: watermark unchanged after rebuild, next incremental pass advanced it normally. Weekly cron Mon 06:50 UTC, with its own heartbeat watched by the EPC monitors.
- **Reconciliation probe** added to the weekly monitor set with the same alerting and heartbeat treatment as the others — the check that would have caught the original failure.
- ⚠️ **Fixes future snapshots, the 9 active clicker segment rules and exports only.** The follow-ups those 2,822 contacts never received, and the wrong-lane messages 1,886 of them did receive, remain **unrecoverable** — those sends happened against frozen pools.
- New convention in 07-conventions: **a passing check is not evidence until you know what it ran against.** Three times this workstream a check was correct while running against the wrong input. Guards must print their input scope by default; an empty scope is a failure, not a pass.

## 2026-08-11 — Creatives lifetime EPC column (sort stays 30-day), offer-report time labelling, sort-whitelist guard — docs: 04-features/epc-denominator, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- **Creatives list + stage picker now show lifetime EPC alongside the 30-day figure**, each with its own click count. `lib/creatives/metrics-cache.ts` gained an unbounded aggregate; measured cost is ~523ms median on a cold read against a 15-minute TTL, so the "roughly doubles it" concern raised when the column was proposed does not matter at this scale.
- **The sort deliberately stays on the 30-day EPC**, and the column now says so (`EPC (30d) ↕`); the lifetime column is explicitly not sortable. The picker decides what gets sent NEXT and recency predicts that better — offers change, audiences fatigue, creative performance decays. Sorting by lifetime would move rankings by a mean of **4.17 places** (max 29, two of the top ten changing), ~3.5x the reshuffle from the denominator change itself. A send-behaviour shift of that size must be a deliberate decision, not a side effect of a display fix.
- **The lifetime column is doing real work:** 14 creatives read $0.00 over 30 days while carrying genuine lifetime revenue — creative 23 shows $0.0000 on 108 recent clickers but $0.5303 across 694 lifetime clickers. An operator can now see that and override deliberately.
- **`/offers/[id]/report` columns are labelled "(all time)"** — labelling only. It applies no date filter because `offer_report_campaign_econ` has no date dimension. Adding one means another migration across two matviews (one already fanning out over `contact_contact_groups`) plus a refresh cron with no error handling, for a gap found by internal audit rather than user demand. Case kept open on [869egyapn](https://app.clickup.com/t/869egyapn).
- **New guard: `scripts/verify-sortable-columns.ts`.** A column declared `enableSorting: true` whose id is missing from the route's server-side whitelist does not fail — the request is accepted, the route falls back to sorting by revenue, and the header still responds. It looks like it works. That happened once while adding the lifetime columns; the check now makes it impossible to reintroduce quietly, and treats a zero-column parse as an error rather than a pass.

## 2026-08-11 — Lifetime EPC displayed as the primary figure (Overview + 4 By-X tabs); remaining gap is TIME BASIS, not denominator — docs: 04-features/epc-denominator, CHANGELOG
- **The spec said lifetime primary everywhere; in practice ZERO surfaces displayed it.** `lifetime_epc` existed in one file (the Overview API) and nothing rendered it. Found by re-checking a claim in an earlier commit message that turned out to be unverified.
- **Size of the gap:** filtering By Offer to 7 days showed six of the eight top campaigns at **$0.00** and one at **$75.00** — a 53x distortion off a single in-window clicker — with no lifetime figure beside them. The timeframe-dependence this workstream set out to remove had simply relocated from "which screen" to "which date filter".
- **Now on Overview and all four By-X tabs:** `Clicks (all time)` · `EPC (all time)` · `Clicks (period)` · `EPC (period)`. Lifetime first and unmuted; period muted alongside. Each EPC sits immediately after the count it divided by — a $0.00 EPC is only interpretable when the denominator beside it reads 4.
- The two are **not derivable from one another** (deduplicated counts are not additive over time), so both are queried and carried separately. By-Group applies the same fractional spread to the lifetime pair as to its period twin — clickers on click weights, revenue on sale weights.
- `/api/keitaro/results` gains an explicit `time_basis` marker: its totals/stages were ALREADY lifetime and merely unlabelled; `rows` are per-day and do not sum to the stage total.
- **Caught while building:** the new Overview columns were rendered with `enableSorting` but the route has a server-side `SORTABLE` whitelist — clicking them would have silently sorted by revenue, with the header responding and the order changing, just not by what was clicked.
- ⚠️ **The remaining defect is TIME BASIS, not denominator.** `/creatives` is 30-day only; `/offers/[id]/report` is lifetime only. Campaign 404 agreeing at $1.4247 across screens is true because those windows happen to line up, NOT because the screens are structurally consistent. Carded: [869egyah0](https://app.clickup.com/t/869egyah0) (creatives — includes the picker-sort decision, a ~3.5x bigger reshuffle than anything shipped so far), [869egyapn](https://app.clickup.com/t/869egyapn) (offer report).

## 2026-08-11 — EPC unification: one click denominator across every screen (migrations 0125, 0126) — docs: 04-features/epc-denominator (new), 03-data-model, 07-conventions, 04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives, CHANGELOG
- **The headline.** Campaign 404 read **$1.0000 on `/creatives` and $12.8049 on `/reports`** — the same campaign, 12.8x apart, depending which page you opened. Both now read **$1.4247**. The old denominators disagreed on **12 of the top 12 campaigns by revenue**; creatives EPC moved UP on 12/12 (its raw-tap denominator was inflated) and reports EPC moved DOWN on 12/12, meeting on one number.
- **All EPC figures across the platform drop by roughly 88%** (platform-wide $7.86 → $0.92). This is the intended correction, not a regression: the old reports denominator was Keitaro OFFER REDIRECTS, one funnel step later than a click and structurally ~8x smaller.
- **The definition.** A counted clicker is a contact with ≥1 click scored `human`, OR a conversion (**Rule F** — so the revenue numerator can never sit outside the click denominator), deduplicated at the grain displayed. The full precedence table is documented even where branches are unreachable (`clicks.classification` has had zero `unknown` rows across all history; "no CamMan row" fires only for manual mode).
- **`counted_clickers` (0125) stores the deduplicated MEMBERSHIP, not counts.** Deduplicated counts are not additive across grains OR over time — one contact clicking on two days is one lifetime clicker, not two — so a count cache could not produce a lifetime figure by summing. One table serves all three grains and both time bases; `first_click_at` doubles as the click-date basis for period revenue.
- ⚠️ **Counted clickers do not sum across grains.** Per-stage and per-creative rows total ~26% above the campaign figure by design (54,494 per campaign-contact vs 68,431 per stage-contact). Documented in the docs AND surfaced in the UI, which now shows the click count beside EPC on every surface.
- **Refresh is tied to the Keitaro poll**, deliberately: revenue advances every 5 min, and a denominator on an independent schedule would make EPC drift between rebuilds and snap back — an artifact indistinguishable from a real trend on the platform's primary metric. Incremental pass rides the poll (~6s, stateless 6h lookback, no watermark); daily full rebuild is the repair path (~46s, `DELETE` not `TRUNCATE`, which would block every EPC read).
- **No watermark, learned from `clickers`:** a watermark makes a derived table silently un-repairable the moment its source is corrected.
- **Freshness is reported as two values** (`updated_at`, `full_rebuild_at`) — a staleness indicator that overstates staleness gets ignored, and is then useless when it is telling the truth.
- **Monitors (weekly)**: human share of taps (±30% MoM, floor 3%, ceiling 25%), excluded-clicker conversion rate (>0.1% — bots do not buy; currently 0.0009% against the 2.24% relay traffic showed pre-fix), Rule F rescue count (>2.5x the baseline of 8), and a precedence row-5 probe (currently 0, so the decision not to build a Keitaro per-click ingest holds). Plus a **mutual dead-man check** — each job watches the other's heartbeat, because a dead job cannot report itself dead.
- **`keitaro_stage_results.epc` removed.** It stored a third definition (revenue ÷ RAW redirects) that nothing read; max value 300.00 shows why it was unusable. The write was removed from the poll FIRST and the column dropped only after a deploy and a clean poll cycle — destructive migrations follow their code, a rule now written into 07-conventions. Pre-drop snapshot of all 7,438 rows in `docs/snapshots/` (checksum `6f9cb5544e86a24f530952bebf48f209`).
- Also corrected: the creatives payout line (revenue ÷ counted clickers, not `Σ sales_count × sales_payout_each`) and the `client.ts` metric label that claimed `campaign_unique_clicks` is "bot/dup-filtered" when CamMan sends no bot filter at all.
- Card: [ClickUp 869egnm4d](https://app.clickup.com/t/869egnm4d).

## 2026-08-11 — Click scoring: exact-ASN matching only + iCloud Private Relay carve-out, with a 4,382-row rescore backfill — docs: 04-features/tracking-attribution (§7a, new), 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- **Found via a metric-integrity check, not a bug report.** 89 converted recipients — **$6,212, 12.1% of attributed revenue** — sat in the revenue numerator but outside the click denominator. Bots don't buy, so the exclusion rule was wrong somewhere.
- **Root cause: substring matching on the ASN organization name** in [`lib/links/datacenter-asns.ts`](../lib/links/datacenter-asns.ts). `"colo"` matched **"NE COLORADO CELLULAR"**, **"COLORADO VALLEY COMMUNICATIONS"** and **"University of Colorado Hospital"**; `"google"` matched **"Google Fiber Inc."** (a residential ISP). Separately, Fastly/Cloudflare/Akamai were listed as datacenter, but they are Apple's three **iCloud Private Relay** egress partners — default-on for iCloud+ subscribers. Every affected click scored +60 → `suspect` → dropped from every click metric. The failure is silent: a false positive is indistinguishable from successful bot filtering.
- **Evidence.** Among CamMan-excluded clickers, relay/CDN + plausible UA converted at **2.2380%** (3,664 clickers, 82 buyers) versus a **0.9703%** benchmark for clickers scored `human`, **0.2241%** for AWS, and **0.0002%** for the Google AS15169 scanner mass (510,679 clickers, 1 buyer). Relay + missing/scanner UA converted at **0.0000%** — which is why the carve-out is relay-ASN **AND** plausible-UA, not relay-ASN alone.
- **Change.** Org-name substring matching deleted outright. The 19 genuine hosting providers it was catching were audited against production traffic (`scripts/probe-keyword-fallback-audit.ts`) and enumerated as explicit ASN numbers, so detection coverage is unchanged. New `CONSUMER_RELAY_ASNS` set (54113 / 13335 / 36183 / 16591) that wins over `DATACENTER_ASNS`, so re-adding one of those numbers by mistake cannot resurrect the bug. `isDatacenterAsn(asn)` no longer takes an org name. Regression guards added to [`scripts/verify-scoring.ts`](../scripts/verify-scoring.ts) for each false positive; full suite green.
- **Backfill (production data write, 2026-08-11): 4,382 rows** — 4,312 `suspect → human`, 70 `bot → suspect`, across 7 ASNs. [`scripts/backfill-rescore-datacenter.ts`](../scripts/backfill-rescore-datacenter.ts) is deterministic and offline (recomputes `is_datacenter` from the already-stored `asn`, re-runs the same pure `scoreClick()`, no MaxMind call), dry-run by default, idempotent (verified: 0 candidates on re-run), and aborts unless the new rule is strictly narrower (verified: 0 rows flip false→true). Pre-write state snapshotted via [`scripts/snapshot-click-classifications.ts`](../scripts/snapshot-click-classifications.ts).
- **Result.** Excluded buyers **89 → 8** ($6,212 → $562; the remainder are AWS stragglers, covered by the forthcoming Rule F converted-floor). Human clickers (campaign grain) 54,500 → **57,576**. Platform `human` taps 79,871 → 84,189.
- ⚠️ **Deliberate in-between state.** Hourly Clickers and By-Group weights read `classification='human'` directly and stepped up immediately; `/reports` EPC still divides by Keitaro redirects until the shared merged-count function ships (held until migrations 0121–0124 clear). The gap is intentional — it keeps the denominator correction separable from the definition change, so a later move has an unambiguous cause. **Any Clickers/By-Group comparison spanning 2026-08-11 is not like-for-like.**
- Part of the EPC-unification workstream — [ClickUp 869egnm4d](https://app.clickup.com/t/869egnm4d).

## 2026-08-10 — Entity names in detail-page tab titles (no migration) — docs: 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- The five entity detail routes showed static titles ("Campaign - Camman"). They now show the record's name ("Summer Promo - Camman"): `campaigns/[id]`, `campaigns/[id]/edit` (`Edit <name>`), `segments/[id]`, `contact-groups/[id]`, `providers/[id]`, `offers/[id]/report`. Each layout swapped static `metadata` for `generateMetadata({ params })` with `params` awaited. Supersedes the previous note that these pages must stay static for want of a cached fetcher.
- 🔒 **Org-scoped by construction.** New [lib/entity-name.ts](../lib/entity-name.ts) holds the single `WHERE id = ? AND org_id = <caller's org>` lookup — the `org_id` predicate is load-bearing, since without it a sequential-id guess would surface another tenant's entity name in the tab. [lib/entity-title.ts](../lib/entity-title.ts) wraps it with org resolution via the already-`React.cache`'d `getUser()`/`getOrgMembership()` (**not** `requireOrgMembership()`, which redirects — metadata must never throw). Malformed id, missing row, blank/NULL name, no session, or wrong org ⇒ static fallback.
- **Cost: one indexed single-row select per detail route**, name column only, no joins. Verified with `pg_stat_statements`: **+1** per detail route, **+1** on `/edit` (the parent layout's `generateMetadata` also runs there — confirmed empirically — and `React.cache` collapses both lookups into one query, which is why `/edit` uses the name rather than discarding it), and **+0** on `/dashboard`, `/campaigns`, `/segments`, `/contacts`, `/reports`.
- **Tests:** [scripts/test-entity-title-tenancy.ts](../scripts/test-entity-title-tenancy.ts) (read-only, 35 assertions — owning org resolves the name, non-owning org gets null, nonexistent/malformed ids null) and [scripts/test-entity-title-render.ts](../scripts/test-entity-title-render.ts) (55 assertions against a running app as `TEST_USER_EMAIL`: rendered `<title>`, fallbacks, no-500, no unauthenticated name leak, query counts). `getEntityName` imports no auth/Next code so the tenancy test runs under `tsx`. Note: renaming an entity in-page does not update the tab until reload — accepted, no client-side syncing.

## 2026-08-10 — Per-route browser tab titles (no migration) — docs: 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- Every route rendered the same `<title>` ("Campaign Manager") because [app/layout.tsx](../app/layout.tsx) was the only place metadata was set. Root now declares `title: { template: "%s - Camman", default: "Camman" }` (strings centralized in new [lib/page-title.ts](../lib/page-title.ts)) and all 35 rendering routes contribute their own bare name. `app/page.tsx` stays untitled — it always `redirect()`s and never paints.
- **6 server pages** got `export const metadata`. **29 client pages** got a sibling title-only `layout.tsx` that returns `children` unchanged — metadata cannot be exported from a `"use client"` module, and no page was converted to a server component. `reports/[dimension]` is the one `generateMetadata` (async `params`, awaited), reading the pure constant `DIMENSION_TAB_LABEL`. **Zero data access added: no new DB query, import, or fetch on any route.**
- **Gotcha found during verification, now documented:** a plain-string `title` in an intermediate layout **nulls the inherited template for every segment below it** — `/campaigns/[id]` rendered a bare `Campaign`, and `/campaigns/[id]/edit` a bare `Edit Campaign`. Segments with titled descendants must re-declare the template, via the new `sectionTitle()` helper; the template also applies to `default`, so baking the suffix in yields `Segments - Camman - Camman`. Six segments need it (`campaigns`, `campaigns/[id]`, `segments`, `contact-groups`, `providers`, `offers`). A **page**'s metadata never cascades, only a **layout**'s.

## 2026-07-31 — Campaign create/activate no longer times out at 60s: prior-offer exclusion split out of the snapshot qualifier — docs: 04-features/audience-snapshot, CLAUDE.md §10b, CHANGELOG
- **Symptom.** "Can't create a campaign" — `Vercel Runtime Timeout Error: Task timed out after 60 seconds` from `POST /api/campaigns` (and the same for draft→active via `POST /api/campaigns/[campaignId]/status`; both call `snapshotAudience` and both set `maxDuration = 60`). Intermittent: some recipes launched in seconds, others died. Four campaign IDs were burned to rolled-back transactions on 2026-07-31 (705, 706, 707, 711) — the gap in `campaigns.id` was what pinned the failure to the create path.
- **Root cause (measured, not inferred).** Inside `snapshotAudience` the content-dedup LAYER 3 exclusion (`exclude_prior_offer_contacts`) was an `oe_set` anti-join in the same statement as the flag joins. `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on the live recipe: `Nested Loop Anti Join … Rows Removed by Join Filter: 115700000`, `Heap Fetches: 115695000`, `Buffers: shared hit=112524958` — `offer_exposures` re-scanned **once per candidate** (23,139 loops × 5,000 rows), **83.8s execution**. Two misestimates compound: the qualifier's outer side estimates `rows=1` (chain of anti-joins over the candidate temp table) and `pg_stats` for `offer_exposures.offer_id` has `n_distinct=8` with MCV `{62,58,80,77,76,83,1,59}` — **offer 79 is absent**, so its 5,000 rows estimate as 1. Both wrong low ⇒ nested loop looks free. Data-shape dependent, which is why it came and went.
- **Fix.** [lib/audience-snapshot.ts](../lib/audience-snapshot.ts): build `audience_qualified` **without** the offer layer, `ANALYZE` it, then apply the exclusion as `DELETE … WHERE EXISTS (offer_exposures …)`. Real stats ⇒ hash anti-join. Same recipe, back-to-back in rolled-back transactions: **67.5s → 4.2s with identical row counts (23,139 both).** All recent live recipes re-timed at **0.9–4.9s** (worst was 68.7s).
- **Not changed.** `previewAudience` keeps the inline `oe_set` join — its aggregate shape does not hit the nested loop (measured 3.9–5.9s on the same recipes). The draft/stage count paths never pass an offer id, so they emit no `oe_set` at all. No schema change, no migration, no data write.
- **Verified.** `scripts/test-snapshot-prior-offer.ts` (preview == frozen snapshot, ON and OFF) identical before/after; `test-segment-exclude`, `test-carrier-rules-filter`, `test-eligible-gate`, `verify-capped-count`, `verify-content-dedup-phase2` all pass; `tsc --noEmit` and `eslint` clean. New [scripts/probe-snapshot-timing.ts](../scripts/probe-snapshot-timing.ts) times `snapshotAudience` for recent campaign recipes in always-rolled-back transactions — re-run it if activation ever feels slow again.

## 2026-07-30 (d) — Phase 2: read-driven in-memory cache for the 30-day creative metrics — docs: creatives-list-slow-stage-picker-2026-07-30, 04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- **Why still needed after the region move.** Colocation removed the round-trip tax but not the work: on `fra1` the picker's `rows` segment was **990 ms of which only ~2 ms was network**. The aggregate is CPU/IO-bound inside Postgres.
- **Indexing rejected ON MEASUREMENT, not assumption.** Candidates built inside a rolled-back transaction against live prod: baseline **1,394 ms**; `links(creative_id)` (the card's literal suggestion) **1,592 ms — worse**; `links(id) INCLUDE (creative_id)` 1,488 ms — worse; best covering variant `links(creative_id, id)` **990 ms** (1.4×) for a **58 MB** index every link insert must maintain. No index helps because the query has **no selective predicate** — it must touch all 566K clicks in the window and map each to a creative.
- **Shape:** [lib/creatives/metrics-cache.ts](../lib/creatives/metrics-cache.ts) — module-level cache keyed by `org_id`, 15-min TTL, single-flight. On a miss it computes both aggregates in ONE round trip (`FULL OUTER JOIN` — a creative can have stage activity with no tracked clicks, or vice versa) and the route injects them as a literal `VALUES` relation, so the SQL shape is unchanged: the four ratios are still computed and **sorted server-side across the whole filtered set**. Ordering and pagination untouched; only the counters' source moved.
- **Deliberately NOT a cache table + refresh cron.** A timer-driven refresh burns DB time whether or not anyone looks — exactly how `report_stage_hour` became the #1 DB consumer with zero readers. This cache is refreshed **by reads**, so it structurally cannot become a dead rollup. It also needs no migration: the unmerged `feat/textrequest-send` branch already claims `0121`–`0124`, and `verify-migration-integrity` validates `snapshot.prevId` by journal index, so a second migration chained off `0120` would break the chain on merge.
- New `mcache` segment in `x-camman-timing` (~0 ms on a hit, full aggregate on a miss) — without it the miss cost was being mis-charged to `enrich`.
- Verification: `scripts/test-creative-metrics-cache.ts` (new) **10/10** — headline assertion compares every served metric against a **live recomputation of the original inline aggregate**, so a silent numeric regression can't hide; plus ratio-derivation, cache-is-consulted, server-side sort, and stable ratio-sort pagination. `test-creatives-list-metrics` 16/16, `test-creatives-api` 49/49, tsc clean, eslint 0 errors.

## 2026-07-30 (c) — ALL functions moved to fra1; `/r/[code]` was the argument FOR, not against — docs: 06-integrations, creatives-list-slow-stage-picker-2026-07-30, CHANGELOG
- **Measured per sequential DB query: `iad1` 187 ms → `fra1` 2.1 ms (89×).** Pure transatlantic round-trip tax, paid on every query, for queries whose server-side execution is often <1 ms. End-to-end on the metrics-free list call: **917 ms → 43 ms (21×)**.
- **The `/r/[code]` objection was wrong, and measurement showed it.** The prior doc asserted US-facing routes had to stay in the US. But the redirect makes **2 sequential DB round trips** (link lookup → click insert; strictly dependent, both awaited) for **0.861 ms** of real DB work (0.469 + 0.392 over 423K calls each) — so from `iad1` it burned ~374 ms of network per click. Nothing is cached or deferred: `force-dynamic`, `/r/` excluded from the proxy matcher (no auth trip), `classifyClick` pure heuristics with scoring deferred to a cron.
- **TLS terminates at the edge PoP, not the function region** — measured TCP connect 14 ms / TLS 68 ms from Poland (would be ~110/330 ms otherwise). So the region move does **not** change handshake cost; only the PoP→function hop moves, trading two transatlantic DB crossings for one. US-handset model: **~400 ms → ~115 ms**.
- **How:** project-level `serverlessFunctionRegion` / `defaultResourceConfig.functionDefaultRegions` → `fra1`. The setting applies to **new deployments only**, so a redeploy is required. Kept **single**-region deliberately — with one DB in `eu-central-1`, extra regions would strand instances transatlantic again.
- Per-route `preferredRegion` remains **dead config** under Fluid Compute (the five existing exports now merely agree with the project setting). Verify placement with `x-vercel-id` = `<edge-pop>::<compute-region>::<id>`; an unauthenticated 401 still executes the function, so any route is a safe probe.

## 2026-07-30 (b) — Post-deploy corrections: Vercel strips `Server-Timing`, and per-route `preferredRegion` has never worked here — docs: 06-integrations, 04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives, creatives-list-slow-stage-picker-2026-07-30, CHANGELOG
- **`Server-Timing` → `x-camman-timing`.** The Phase-0 attribution header shipped that morning worked locally but was **absent in prod** — Vercel's edge strips `Server-Timing` before it reaches the client (confirmed by dumping response headers against `camman.vercel.app`). Renamed to a custom `x-` header, which passes through untouched. Cost: no native DevTools Timing-tab rendering; read it in Network → Headers.
- **⚠️ `preferredRegion` is dead config on this project.** The `fra1` pin bundled that morning was measured and **removed the same day**. The project has **Fluid Compute** enabled (`defaultResourceConfig.fluid: true`), so function placement comes from the project-level `functionDefaultRegions` (`["iad1"]`) and per-route segment exports are **ignored**. Diagnostic needing no deploy: `x-vercel-id` = `<edge-pop>::<compute-region>::<id>`, and an unauthenticated 401 still executes the function — `/api/clicks/score-pending` and `/api/opt-outs/poll`, **pinned to fra1 since 2026-07-13**, both report `iad1`, identically to a never-pinned control route. **So the 2026-07-13 change made to stop two cron routes timing out at the 60s cap never actually moved them.** The five existing pins are left in place as dead config; `06-integrations.md` now warns against adding more. The only real lever is the project-level region setting, which moves every function including `/r/[code]` (US-handset SMS redirect) — an infra decision for the owner, not a code change.

## 2026-07-30 — Slow stage creative loading: metrics opt-out, dead rollup cron retired, 2 correctness fixes — docs: creatives-list-slow-stage-picker-2026-07-30 (new), 04-features/campaigns-stages-creatives, 04-features/crons, 04-features/reports-rollup, 06-integrations, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- **Root cause (not creative count).** `/api/creatives/list` joined an **org-wide** 30-day click aggregate — `clicks` (566K rows/30d) ⨝ `links` (1.84M rows / 319 MB heap, **no index on `links.creative_id`**) — that is *not* restricted to the returned page, so `LIMIT` can't prune it. Measured **2,567 ms** total with **2,504 ms (97.5%)** in that one branch and **499,566 buffers**; the same query without metrics is **2.9 ms / 14 buffers**. `pg_stat_statements`: 929 calls, mean ~1.2 s, max 5.7 s, **~31,000 blocks (242 MB) read from disk per call**, 1,126 s cumulative. It never caches because `shared_buffers` is 512 MB against a 319 MB heap and Postgres ring-buffers large seq scans. Payload was never a factor (all 180 active creatives = **15 kB** of text). `creatives` is a 193-row table — the "many creatives" correlation is incidental.
- **Phase 1 — `include_metrics`.** New query param, **defaulting to `true`** (creatives list page + picker unaffected). The stage form's creative dropdown — whose `Creative` type has no `metrics` field and which was therefore computing the aggregate and discarding it — now passes `include_metrics=false`. Measured `rows` **1,997 ms → 57 ms**, end-to-end **2,835 ms → 316 ms**. When off the `metrics` key is **omitted, not zero-filled**; a ratio sort with metrics off degrades to `created_at` instead of erroring.
- **Phase 0 — `Server-Timing`.** Vercel retains no runtime logs without a log drain, so the route now emits `auth`/`rows`/`enrich`/`total` + a `metrics;desc="1|0"` marker, readable in DevTools → Network → Timing. Notably, with metrics off `auth`+`enrich` (~230 ms) dominate the remaining total — the query stops being the bottleneck, which is the data that should decide whether Phase 2 (a metrics cache table) is needed at all. **Phase 2 deliberately not built.**
- **Phase 3 — dead rollup cron retired.** `/api/cron/report-rollup` removed from `vercel.json` (route kept, callable manually). `report_stage_hour`/`report_group_hour` are **write-only**: `/reports` + Overview source `lib/reporting/stage-funnel.ts`, Telegram uses its own queries, repo-wide grep finds no `SELECT`, and `pg_stat_user_tables` shows `idx_scan` (1,492,814) ≈ `n_tup_upd` (1,491,062) — the apparent reads were the upsert's own `ON CONFLICT` probes. At `14,29,44,59` it was the **#1 and #2 query by total DB time in the whole database** (3.75 hours cumulative, ~355 MB/burst). Tables left in place; dropping them needs a migration + card.
- **Phase 4a — silent truncation (correctness).** `parseListParams` clamped `pageSize` to 100 while the picker requested 200, so an org with >100 eligible active creatives lost the tail with no indication. Added opt-in `parseListParams(req, { maxPageSize })` (500 here); the picker now requests 500 **and warns** when `data.length < totalCount`.
- **Phase 4b — non-deterministic ordering (correctness, found by the new test).** The default sort branch had no `id` tiebreaker although the ratio and `spam_score` branches did and the adjacent comment already claimed pagination was deterministic. Bulk-create commits a batch in one transaction, so **up to 7 active creatives share a `created_at` to the microsecond** — a paginated list could show one twice and skip another. One-line fix.
- Also: `preferredRegion = "fra1"` on `/api/creatives/list` (~5 sequential round-trips/request). Applied as the established **per-route** segment export, **not** a global `regions` field in `vercel.json` — a global pin would drag `/r/[code]`, the US-handset SMS redirect, away from its users.
- Verification: `scripts/test-creatives-list-metrics.ts` (new) 16/16; `scripts/test-creatives-api.ts` 49/49; `tsc --noEmit` clean; eslint 0 errors (8 warnings, all pre-existing on `main`).

## 2026-07-26 — Prepare progress counter reported prior-run audit rows (follow-up to #27) — docs: 04-features/daily-volume-ui, CHANGELOG
- The Prepare dialog's progress poll ([…/send/materialize-progress](<../app/api/campaigns/[campaignId]/stages/[stageId]/send/materialize-progress/route.ts>)) counted `stage_sends` with **no status filter**, so re-preparing a previously-aborted stage counted the prior run's cancelled rows as progress for the current one. Measured on the two stages re-prepared this morning: stage 1710 reported **8,843** against 4,445 real recipients and stage 1713 **18,432** against 9,243 — roughly 2×, with the bar starting near-full before a single new row existed.
- Fix: `AND ss.status <> 'rejected'` — one line, read-only route, no migration. `rejected` is written **only** by the abort path, so it is unambiguously prior-run. `skipped_opted_out` is deliberately **not** excluded: those rows are legitimately created *by* the run in progress (migration 0116) and counting them keeps the bar tracking the preflight target. The only residue that survives is a prior run's suppression rows — 2 on stage 1710, 10 on 1713 — against the 4,396/9,179 the filter removes.
- This was the last reader still treating audit rows as an audience, closing the invariant established in the 2026-07-26 abort fix below.
- Verified: `tsc` clean, `eslint` clean, new `scripts/test-materialize-progress-count.ts` **14/14** (covers mid-run progress, a suppression recorded during the run, completion, and a never-aborted stage as a regression guard). Read-only against production, the fixed query reads **4,447** (stage 1710) and **9,253** (stage 1713) versus 8,843 / 18,432 before. (ClickUp 869e9dq69.)

## 2026-07-26 — Aborted stage stuck on Indigo "Materializing" with no Prepare button — docs: 04-features/sms-send-pipeline, 04-features/daily-volume-ui, CHANGELOG
- **Symptom.** Production stages 1713 (Glyco Balance, campaign 465) and 1710 (Lulutox, campaign 467) were aborted, then sat permanently on the Indigo "Materializing" badge with the Prepare action hidden — unrecoverable through the product.
- **Cause.** `…/send/abort` cancels the *pending* rows to `rejected` and resets `materialized_at = NULL`, but deliberately keeps the `skipped_opted_out` audit rows written at materialization time (migration 0116). Every `stage_sends` count query excluded only `rejected` from `total`, so an aborted stage still reported `total > 0` (9 rows for 1713, 2 for 1710). That satisfied both `deriveStageOperationalStatus`'s `materializedAt == null && total > 0` → `materializing`, and `hasBatch = counts.total > 0` in [stage-send-panel.tsx](../components/campaigns/stage-send-panel.tsx), which hides Prepare. The 2026-07-17 cancel fix had the right intent — its comment says excluding `rejected` exists so a canceled stage "reads total=0 → … NOT a stuck Materializing" — it just missed the second audit bucket.
- **Fix (no migration, no data surgery).** `total` now excludes **both** audit buckets (`status NOT IN ('rejected','skipped_opted_out')`) in all four surfaces: [stages](<../app/api/campaigns/[campaignId]/stages/route.ts>), [send panel](<../app/api/campaigns/[campaignId]/stages/[stageId]/send/route.ts>), [/sends/today](../app/api/sends/today/route.ts), [/sends/autopilot](../app/api/sends/autopilot/route.ts). As a structural backstop, [deriveStageOperationalStatus](../lib/stages/stage-status.ts) now decides "materializing" on **live rows** (`pending + sending + sent`) rather than row count, so a future audit-only status cannot re-strand a stage the same way; with nothing live and nothing materialized a stage reads `scheduled_unprepared`/`draft` and offers Prepare. The invariant is documented at the source in the abort route: audit rows are kept on purpose, so no reader may treat them as an audience.
- **Test gap closed.** `scripts/test-cancel-rematerialize.ts` materialized only `pending` rows, so its cancelled stage had zero non-rejected rows and the bug was invisible. The fixture now suppresses rows to `skipped_opted_out` first, reproducing the real signature. Also documents a behavior worth knowing: the `status <> 'rejected'` resumability filter in [recipients.ts](../lib/sends/recipients.ts) keeps opted-out contacts excluded on re-enumeration (correct — re-adding them would re-target someone who asked to stop), so a re-prepared stage is legitimately smaller than the original by the suppressed count.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `test-stage-status` 16/16 (6 new cases); `test-cancel-rematerialize` 27/27; read-only `scripts/verify-aborted-stage-prepare.ts` confirms stages 1713/1710 go `total` 9→0 and 2→0, `hasBatch` true→false, Prepare hidden→shown with no data change. (ClickUp 869e9dq69.)
## 2026-07-26 — Per-phone drain lease (overlapping `send-scheduled` invocations) — docs: 04-features/sms-send-pipeline, 04-features/crons, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- `/api/cron/send-scheduled` took **no** cron lease, so a tick that overran its 300s `maxDuration` overlapped the next one and **two drain loops sent on the same number simultaneously**. Per-second pacing (`provider_phones.max_sends_per_second`) is enforced *within* one invocation, so N overlapping invocations multiply the real MPS by N — a live carrier-compliance exposure, safe only by arithmetic accident (2 concurrent loops measured ~40/s on a 60/s-configured short code; the same overlap on a 3/s toll-free would breach it). Fix: Phase B wraps each phone group in a **per-PHONE** lease, `withKeyedLease(dbc, "send-drain:p<phoneId>", PHASE_B_DEADLINE_MS, …)` — new [lib/cron/keyed-lease.ts](../lib/cron/keyed-lease.ts) (the claim/release core, extracted from [lib/cron/lease.ts](../lib/cron/lease.ts), which now delegates; the core is intentionally NOT `server-only` and takes the db/tx handle as a parameter so the `tsx` test harnesses can exercise it inside a rolled-back transaction).
- **Per phone, not per job** — different numbers still drain in parallel, so the 2026-07-24 head-of-line fix is untouched; stages with no `provider_phone_id` key on their own stage id. A `cron_locks` lease **row**, not `pg_advisory_lock` (session locks are unsafe through the `:6543` transaction pooler; a txn-scoped one would pin the whole multi-minute drain to a single connection, defeating the per-window-commit resumable design). Crash-safe by absolute expiry — TTL = `PHASE_B_DEADLINE_MS` (240s) provably covers the work (deadline measured from **run** start, lease from **group** start ≥ run start), released in `finally` via CAS, and one cron period (300s) > TTL so the next tick always reclaims a killed run's phone. A held lease **skips cleanly**: rows stay `pending`, nothing marked `schedule_missed_at`, `sent_at` unstamped, no error escapes; counted as the new `phone_lease_skipped` on `ScheduledRunResult`. Applies to the **manual** POST trigger too (unlike the job-wide `withCronLease` convention) — an MPS breach doesn't care who started the drain. No schema change (`cron_locks` already exists, migration 0103). Verified by new [scripts/test-phone-drain-lease.ts](../scripts/test-phone-drain-lease.ts) (18 checks) + [scripts/verify-drain.ts](../scripts/verify-drain.ts) + [scripts/test-scheduled-phone-concurrency.ts](../scripts/test-scheduled-phone-concurrency.ts), `tsc --noEmit` clean. (ClickUp 869e9dq6f.)
## 2026-07-26 — Opt-out-rate breaker: aligned send cohort, per-stage scope, visible campaign pause (no migration) — docs: 04-features/sms-send-pipeline, 04-features/crons, 05-flows, 06-integrations, 07-conventions, superpowers/specs, CHANGELOG
- **Fixes the false-trip defect diagnosed 2026-07-25** ([brief](optout-rate-breaker-false-trip-2026-07-25.md)). The breaker divided STOPs counted by **arrival** time by messages counted by **send** time — different cohorts, so the ratio was unbounded and four campaigns auto-paused at a reported 15–37% against true per-stage rates of 0.75–2.80%. The numerator now reaches its time bucket through the send that produced it (`opt_out_attributions oa JOIN stage_sends ss ON ss.id = oa.stage_send_id`, bucketed by `ss.sent_at`), so both sides describe the same messages. Rows with a NULL `stage_send_id` are excluded with **no `stage_id` fallback** (a fallback reintroduces receipt-time bucketing for exactly those rows); verified 0/43,487 affected all-time.
- **Per-STAGE evaluation, per-CAMPAIGN latch.** `checkOptOutRateBreaker` takes `stageId` and judges the attributed stage only — no fan-out (a STOP credits one stage; other stages' denominators only grow, which lowers a rate). Reason string names both: `optout_rate_spike: 12.4% (62/500) on stage 1713 over 24h`. Callers updated: [poll-opt-outs.ts](../lib/sends/poll-opt-outs.ts), [ahoi-optout.ts](../lib/sends/ahoi-optout.ts).
- **Short-window twin** so a cohort rate's inherent lag doesn't slow real detection: 2h @ 8% (`OPTOUT_RATE_WINDOW_SHORT_SEC` / `OPTOUT_RATE_SPIKE_THRESHOLD_SHORT` / `OPTOUT_RATE_MIN_SENDS_SHORT`) alongside the unchanged 24h @ 10%. The short window is a strict subset, so both counts come from **one query per side** via `FILTER` — per-STOP query count stays at 2. `tripped_by: "24h" | "2h" | null`. Recalibrated on the aligned cohort (318 stages ≥200 sends: 24h p95 7.19% / max 8.41%; 2h p95 5.23% / max 6.12%) — each threshold sits above the observed maximum. Decision logic extracted as the pure `decideOptOutRateBreaker`.
- **Null-`stage_send_id` blind-spot watch** — `findUnjoinableOptOutAttributions` + `formatUnjoinableAlert` ([unjoinable-attributions.ts](../lib/sends/unjoinable-attributions.ts)), called hourly from [`/api/cron/telegram-report`](../app/api/cron/telegram-report/route.ts) beside the stall detector; alerts >5% on ≥20 rows. Deliberately not on the hot path.
- **The campaign pause is now visible.** New `blocked` stage operational status (rose, `willSend: attention`) in [stage-status.ts](../lib/stages/stage-status.ts), derived from a new `campaignSendPaused` input checked after `scheduleMissedAt` and before `slipHoldAt`, and only for stages with outstanding work. [/api/sends/today](../app/api/sends/today/route.ts) + [/api/sends/autopilot](../app/api/sends/autopilot/route.ts) select the already-joined `c.send_paused`/`_reason`/`_at` (no extra query) and return a top-level `paused_campaigns[]` from the shared pure `summarizePausedCampaigns` ([paused-campaigns.ts](../lib/sends/paused-campaigns.ts)). `/sends/today` gains a banner with per-campaign Resume (perm `campaigns.pause`), a red *campaign paused* chip, and a `· held by campaign pause` marker; `/sends/autopilot` gains the chip + Resume in its action column.
- **Resume + re-date in ONE transaction.** `POST /api/campaigns/[campaignId]/send-circuit` accepts `redate_stages: [{stage_id, scheduled_at}]`; a bad entry throws so the whole recovery rolls back. New `GET` on the same route classifies unfired stages via `decideScheduledSend` (`fire`/`hold`/`future`/`missed`/`unscheduled`) with `suggested_scheduled_at` from `nextWindowOpenAtOrAfter`, driving the new `<CampaignResumeDialog>` ([components/sends/campaign-resume-dialog.tsx](../components/sends/campaign-resume-dialog.tsx), a `<FormDialog>`). Needed because the send window is anchored to `scheduled_at`'s ET day, so resuming after that day's close would stamp `schedule_missed_at` instead of sending. Re-dates route through `decideScheduleEdit` + `isScheduledAtInPast`; held stages stay un-missed.
- **No migration** — every column already existed. Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `scripts/test-optout-breaker-decision.ts` **46/46** offline (incl. all four incident campaigns' exact shapes not tripping); `scripts/verify-optout-breaker-alignment.ts` (read-only, live) — **0 of 29** stages that sent in the last 24h would trip, and 0/37,661 attributions unjoinable over 30 days. `scripts/test-optout-rate-breaker.ts` rewritten (its old fixture inserted attributions with a NULL `stage_send_id`, which the aligned join correctly counts as zero) — needs a DB run.

## 2026-07-25 — Opt-out-rate breaker false-trip diagnostic (investigation only, no code change) — docs: optout-rate-breaker-false-trip-2026-07-25 (new), CHANGELOG
- Read-only diagnosis of four campaigns (463/464/465/467) auto-paused between 10:51 and 11:33 ET on false signals, ~26,500 messages stranded. Root cause: the breaker's numerator and denominator were measured on different cohorts (STOP arrival time vs message send time), making the computed "rate" unbounded. Also documented three collateral issues: a paused campaign's due stages fall into a silent limbo (never drained, never marked missed), the pause is invisible on both operational dashboards, and resuming after the send window closes marks stages missed. Fixed 2026-07-26 (above).
## 2026-07-26 — Phone edit dialog silently dropped `max_sends_per_second` + `dashboard_id`; MPS now shown in the phones table — docs: 04-features/sms-send-pipeline, CHANGELOG
- **Write bug (no migration).** `submitPhonePatch` in [app/(protected)/providers/[id]/page.tsx](<../app/(protected)/providers/[id]/page.tsx>) hand-built its PATCH body from a hardcoded `{cost_per_sms, brand_id}` whitelist dating to the original providers commit — it predates both `max_sends_per_second` (migration 0073) and `dashboard_id` (migration 0120). Both fields were collected by the form, accepted by `providerPhoneUpdateSchema`, and honored by the route's generic update loop, but were **dropped client-side before the fetch**; the PATCH returned 200 and the success toast fired while the column never changed. Only the *create* path ever persisted a rate. Fixed by widening the whitelist and sending `?? null` (not `undefined`, which `JSON.stringify` omits) so clearing an input clears the column. No server, validator, or schema change was needed.
- **Impact.** Unset numbers fall back to `DEFAULT_SENDS_PER_SECOND` (10). That under-sends a 60/s short code but is carrier-safe; the real hazard was the reverse — an operator *lowering* a rate (e.g. a 3/s toll-free) had the edit silently dropped and the drain kept pacing at 10/s into a 3/s limit. Text Request phone 114 was left at NULL rate **and** NULL `dashboard_id`, which the UI could not set at all for an already-created number — an unlisted blocker for the `txr` `supports_api_send` flip.
- **Display.** The phones table had no MPS column at all, so even a correctly-created value was invisible outside the Edit dialog. Added a sortable **Max / sec** column rendering `10 (default)` for NULL, plus `max_sends_per_second` in the phones list route's `SORT_COLUMNS` whitelist — unlisted sort keys silently fall back to `created_at`, so a sortable column without a whitelist entry renders a dead control.
- Verified: `tsc --noEmit` clean; `eslint` identical to the pre-change baseline (9 problems, all pre-existing). Read-only prod probe [scripts/probe-provider-mps-recon.ts](../scripts/probe-provider-mps-recon.ts) confirmed 5/34 phones carry a rate (short codes correctly at 60) and that the drain reads the same column the UI writes ([lib/sends/drain.ts](../lib/sends/drain.ts) `LEFT JOIN provider_phones` → `resolveSendsPerSecond`). (ClickUp 869e9dq6b.)
## 2026-07-25 — Text Request (`txr`) Phases 2–4: send, DLR, backstops, opt-out intake (migrations 0121–0124) — docs: 03-data-model (+ERD), 04-features/sms-send-pipeline, 04-features/crons, 05-flows (E6, E7), 06-integrations, 07-conventions, CHANGELOG
- **Phase 2 — send.** Real `send()`: `POST /messages` `{from,to,body,status_callback?}` with the `x-api-key` header, classified on BOTH the HTTP status and the body `status` (TR returns real status codes, unlike Ahoi's always-200). Opt-out codes 2100/30050 ⇒ `suppressed` ⇒ recorded `filtered`. Captures `segments_count` → new nullable `send_attempts.segments_count` (**migration 0121**). `withProviderFooter`/`TXR_APPENDED_FOOTER` teach the G8 kickoff gate that TR **auto-appends `\nText STOP to opt out` server-side**, so a body that fits N segments raw can tip to N+1 on the wire.
- **Phase 3a — real-time DLR.** **Migration 0122** `textrequest_dlr_events` + `lib/sends/textrequest-dlr.ts` (parse/capture/reconcile) + `app/api/webhooks/textrequest/status/[token]/route.ts`. Reconcile is DIRECT via the callback URL's `?ss=<stage_send_id>` (TR's advantage over Ahoi's uuid-guessing), falling back to `message_id` → `stage_sends.texthub_message_id`. The drain threads the per-message callback URL for `txr` only; a missing `inbound_webhook_token` or origin ⇒ no callback requested (the poll then reconciles).
- **Phase 3b — backstops + webhook health.** New cron `/api/cron/textrequest-poll` (`4,19,34,49`, `withCronLease`) running: the **messages poll** (`GET /dashboards/{id}/messages`, documented `start_date`/`end_date`), idempotent per **migration 0123**'s poll-only partial unique index; the **contacts poll**; and **webhook health** — TR disconnects a hook after 10 consecutive non-2XX/3XX (`is_connected:false`, silent), so every tick reactivates ours via `PUT` and alerts. Poll targets come from `provider_phones` bound to a txr credential WITH a `dashboard_id`, never "all dashboards on the account". Messages are always oldest→newest with no honored sort param, so the poll reads page 0 for `total_items` then walks pages **backwards** — a page cap then drops the oldest, not the newest, and is reported (`truncated` + Telegram), never silent.
- **Phase 4 — opt-out intake (compliance-critical).** **Migration 0124** `textrequest_inbound_events` (six `source` channels) + `lib/sends/textrequest-optout.ts`'s `processTextrequestOptOut`, a deliberate mirror of `processAhoiInboundOptOut` (contact upsert → `opt_outs` → cascade-cancel pending `stage_sends` → attribution via the shared `latestSendForAttribution` → stage counters + `recomputeStageTotalCost` → `checkOptOutRateBreaker`). Four signals feed it: `msg_received` STOP, `contact_updated` (`opted_out_utc`/`is_suppressed`), the two polls, and errorCode **2100** (delivery status) / **30050** (send reject). New account-webhook route `app/api/webhooks/textrequest/events/[token]` (handles all three subscribed events, ALWAYS 200 so a processing failure can't trip TR's 10-strike disconnect — it alerts to Telegram instead) and an operator-triggered `POST .../credentials/[credentialId]/register-textrequest-hooks` that registers the hooks and mints the per-credential `inbound_webhook_token`. Capture is idempotent **across channels** via UNIQUE(`provider_id`,`provider_uuid`) — webhook and poll share TR's message GUID; a 45-min window dedups cross-SHAPE duplicates.
